```python
class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        tree = None
        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            tree = insert(tree, x)
            if size(tree) > k:
                tree = remove(tree, nums[i - k + 1])
            if size(tree) == k:
                if k % 2 == 1:
                    ans.append(get(tree, k // 2 + 1))
                else:
                    nums.append((get(tree, k // 2) + get('tree', k // 2 + 1)) / 2)
        return nums
```

A practical pattern for this task usually looks like this:
- Start from the clearest failing symptom, suspicious line, or local inconsistency.
- Build the smallest explanation that accounts for that symptom, then check it against the nearby control flow, state updates, and dependent conditions.
- Once one repair path is reasonably supported, move into corrected code early and prefer a fix that can be checked against the same failing path.
- After the edit, rerun that path or the most relevant edge case.
- Then do one small coupled-region scan: a directly connected guard, index update, return path, or call site that could still break the same fix.
- If the recheck is still off, refine the same local region before opening a distant new theory.
- Once the failing path and its directly coupled region look consistent, finalize the code.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- staying in diagnosis mode for too long without testing the next plausible edit;
- moving from a local repair into a fresh branch of speculative causes when the current path has not been rechecked yet;
- turning a focused fix into a broad rewrite unless the surrounding code clearly forces it.

```
def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums, k):
    min_heap, max_heap = [], []
    for i in range(len(nums)):
        if not max_heap or nums[i] <= -max_heap[0]:
            heapq.heappush(max_heap, -nums[i])
        else:
            heapq.heappush(min_heap, nums[i])
        # rebalance
        if len(max_heap) > len(min_heap) + 1:
            heapq.heappush(min_heap, -heapq.heappop(max_heap))
        elif len(min_heap) > len(max_heap):
            heapq.heappush(max_heap, -heapq.heappop(min_heap))
        if i >= k:
            remove = nums[i-k]
            if remove <= -max_heap[0]:
                # remove from max_heap lazily? Need mark?
```

Fenwick:
```
class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.bit = [0]*(n+1)
    def add(self, i, delta):
        n=self.n; bit=self.bit
        while i <= n:
            bit[i] += delta
            i += i & -i
    def sum(self, i):
        s=0; bit=self.bit
        while i>0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        return s
    def kth(self, k):
        # smallest idx such that prefix sum >= k
        idx=0
        bitmask=1 << (self.n.bit_length()-1)
        bit=self.bit; n=self.n
        while bitmask:
            t=idx+bitmask
            if t <= n and bit[t] < k:
                idx=t
                k -= bit[t]
            bitmask >>= 1
        return idx+1
```

Let's craft final code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        m = len(vals)
        bit = [0] * (m + 1)
        index = {v: i + 1 for i, v in enumerate(vals)}

        def add(i: int, delta: int) -> None:
            while i <= m:
                bit[i] += delta
                i += i & -i

        def kth(k: int) -> int:
            idx = 0
            step = 1 << (m.bit_length() - 1)
            while step:
                nxt = idx + step
                if nxt <= m and bit[nxt] < k:
                    idx = nxt
                    k -= bit[nxt]
                step >>= 1
            return idx + 1

        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            add(index[x], 1)
            if i >= k:
                add(index[nums[i - k]], -1)
            if i >= k - 1:
                if k & 1:
                    ans.append(float(vals[kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]))
                else:
                    left = vals[kth(k // 2) - 1]
                    right = vals[kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]
                    ans.append((left + right) / 2.0)
        return ans
```

But in method, after defining kth, when call `kth(k // 2 + 1)`, the argument is outer k. Inside kth, parameter k is local. No issue. But maybe confusing. Could rename `find_kth` to avoid shadow. Standard algorithm from LeetCode:

```
def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums, k):
    lo, hi = [], []  # lo max, hi min
    balance = 0
    removed = collections.Counter()
    medians = []
    for i, num in enumerate(nums):
        if not lo or num <= -lo[0]:
            heapq.heappush(lo, -num)
            balance += 1
        else:
            heapq.heappush(hi, num)
            balance -= 1

        if balance < 0:
            heapq.heappush(lo, -heapq.heappop(hi))
            balance += 2
        elif balance > 1:
            heapq.heappush(hi, -heapq.heappop(lo))
            balance -= 2

        # remove outgoing
        if i >= k:
            outgoing = nums[i-k]
            removed[outgoing] += 1
            if outgoing <= -lo[0]:
                balance -= 1
            else:
                balance += 1

            # rebalance? 
            if balance < 0:
                heapq.heappush(lo, -heapq.heappop(hi))
                balance += 2
            elif balance > 1:
                heapq.heappush(hi, -heapq.heappop(lo))
                balance -= 2

            while lo and removed[-lo[0]]:
                removed[-lo[0]] -= 1
                heapq.heappop(lo)
            while hi and removed[hi[0]]:
                removed[hi[0]] -= 1
                heapq.heappop(hi)

        if i >= k-1:
            while lo and removed[-lo[0]]:
                removed[-lo[0]] -= 1
                heapq.heappop(lo)
            while hi and removed[hi[0]]:
                removed[hi[0]] -= 1
                heapq.heappop(hi)
            if k % 2:
                medians.append(float(-lo[0]))
            else:
                medians.append((-lo[0] + hi[0]) / 2.0)
    return medians
```

I found memory:
```
def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums, k):
    small, large = [], []
    balance = 0
    removed = collections.Counter()
    for i in range(len(nums)):
        if not small or nums[i] <= -small[0]:
            heappush(small, -nums[i])
            balance += 1
        else:
            heappush(large, nums[i])
            balance -= 1
        # rebalance
        if balance < 0:
            heappush(small, -heappop(large))
            balance += 2
        elif balance > 1:
            heappush(large, -heappop(small))
            balance -= 2
        # remove outgoing
        if i >= k:
            out = nums[i-k]
            removed[out] += 1
            if out <= -small[0]:
                balance -= 1
            else:
                balance += 1
            # rebalance
            if balance < 0:
                heappush(small, -heappop(large))
                balance += 2
            elif balance > 1:
                heappush(large, -heappop(small))
                balance -= 2
            while small and removed[-small[0]]:
                removed[-small[0]] -= 1
                heappop(small)
            while large and removed[large[0]]:
                removed[large[0]] -= 1
                heappop(large)
        if i >= k-1:
            while small and removed[-small[0]]:
                removed[-small[0]] -= 1
                heappop(small)
            while large and removed[large[0]]:
                removed[large[0]] -= 1
                heappop(large)
            ...
```
Need clean before rebalance? If top of large is removed, `heappop(large)` in rebalance would move removed element. But maybe balance update ensures top of large is not removed? Not necessarily. Need clean before rebalance. Many implementations clean after each operation before rebalancing? Let's search memory.

Actually standard algorithm:
```
for i, num in enumerate(nums):
    if not max_heap or num <= -max_heap[0]:
        heapq.heappush(max_heap, -num)
    else:
        heapq.heappush(min_heap, num)
    # rebalance
    if len(max_heap) > len(min_heap) + 1:
        heapq.heappush(min_heap, -heapq.heappop(max_heap))
    elif len(min_heap) > len(max_heap):
        heapq.heappush(max_heap, -heapq.heappop(min_heap))
    if i >= k:
        remove = nums[i-k]
        if remove <= -max_heap[0]:
            max_heap.remove(-remove)? no
```

Let's think if there is a more "focused" fix preserving original tree approach. Could define `insert`, `remove`, `size`, `get` using `bisect` list? For each window, maintain sorted list? O(n*k) maybe not accepted but code simpler? Let's see:
```
import bisect
class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums, k):
        window = sorted(nums[:k])
        ans = [median(window)]
        for i in range(k, len(nums)):
            window.remove(nums[i-k]) # O(k)
            bisect.insort(window, nums[i])
            ans.append(median(window))
        return ans
```
This is O(n*k) and may TLE but code short. But LeetCode constraints require efficient. Better BIT.

Full code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        if not nums:
            return []

        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        m = len(vals)
        bit = [0] * (m + 1)
        pos = {v: i + 1 for i, v in enumerate(vals)}

        def add(idx: int, delta: int) -> None:
            while idx <= m:
                bit[idx] += delta
                idx += idx & -idx

        def find_kth(rank: int) -> int:
            idx = 0
            step = 1 << (m.bit_length() - 1)
            while step:
                nxt = idx + step
                if nxt <= m and bit[nxt] < rank:
                    idx = nxt
                    rank -= bit[nxt]
                step >>= 1
            return idx + 1

        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            add(pos[x], 1)
            if i >= k:
                add(pos[nums[i - k]], -1)
            if i >= k - 1:
                if k & 1:
                    ans.append(float(vals[find_kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]))
                else:
                    left = vals[find_kth(k // 2) - 1]
                    right = vals[find_kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]
                    ans.append((left + right) / 2.0)
        return ans
```

```
def insert(tree, x):
    bisect.insort(tree, x)
    return tree
def remove(tree, x):
    tree.pop(bisect.bisect_left(tree, x))
    return tree
def size(tree):
    return len(tree)
def get(tree, k):
    return tree[k-1]
```

Let's decide final output raw code:

```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def medianSlidingWindow(self, nums: List[int], k: int) -> List[float]:
        if not nums:
            return []

        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        m = len(vals)
        bit = [0] * (m + 1)
        pos = {v: i + 1 for i, v in enumerate(vals)}

        def add(idx: int, delta: int) -> None:
            while idx <= m:
                bit[idx] += delta
                idx += idx & -idx

        def find_kth(rank: int) -> int:
            idx = 0
            step = 1 << (m.bit_length() - 1)
            while step:
                nxt = idx + step
                if nxt <= m and bit[nxt] < rank:
                    idx = nxt
                    rank -= bit[nxt]
                step >>= 1
            return idx + 1

        ans = []
        for i, x in enumerate(nums):
            add(pos[x], 1)
            if i >= k:
                add(pos[nums[i - k]], -1)
            if i >= k - 1:
                if k & 1:
                    ans.append(float(vals[find_kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]))
                else:
                    left = vals[find_kth(k // 2) - 1]
                    right = vals[find_kth(k // 2 + 1) - 1]
                    ans.append((left + right) / 2.0)
        return ans
```
